This is, of course, related to the diagnosis of the situation and to the diagnosis of the challenges facing the world, including Poland, in terms of innovation. Today, when we are at the threshold of the fourth industrial revolution in the economy, based on knowledge and data, in which robotics, the development of artificial intelligence, energy storage are to play a key role, we understand that all these aspects will radically change the economic image of the world, including Poland. As far as tax matters are concerned, the answer is the responsibility of the Minister of Finance. We as the Ministry of Entrepreneurship and Technology certainly want to maintain the limit of 30 times the minimum wage when calculating the pension contribution. I would like to say that today we see that at this moment Poland is an absolute leader in the FinTech industry and everything that involves it. According to Global Financial Centres Index rankings, which examine the competitiveness of major financial centres, we are ranked 45th in the world, in Europe - 12. We are the leading financial centre in Central and Eastern Europe. Doing Business 2017 ranking Poland ranks high, 24th place. In turn, the report of the Basel Financial Supervision Committee shows that Poland is one of the 14 countries most supporting the FinTech industry. In Poland, global banks such as Credit Suisse, UBS and Citi were located in their support and outsourcing centres. I say this not only to express my pride, but above all to say that we understand that the world is changing very quickly and it is our job to maintain this position and even to gain an ever better position. That's why we're investigating all kinds of legal acts. The Innovation Act itself introduced improvements in the legal order of abolishing income tax on intellectual and industrial property imports, introduced tax incentives for R &amp; D, gradually extending the list of eligible costs, extended the period during which an entrepreneur can deduct costs incurred for R &amp; D activities from 3 to 6 years, and introduced the possibility of cash-back for newly established R &amp; D companies. We are also preparing changes related to the initiative of a simple public limited liability company, which allows for an intellectual contribution, calculated as well as a financial contribution, highlighting its importance. I would also like to say that I have commissioned the Department of Economic Analysis, which in the Ministry of Entrepreneurship and Technology oversees the continuous monitoring of the development of the industry to be followed by what solutions are being put in place in the world. We are ready and open to dialogue with the industry to create the best possible conditions. In conclusion, I would like to mention one more thing. There was a question of recognising that the creation of computer programs is a research and development activity. In Poland, this is already possible in many areas. We would have to talk about details, but already there are entities that are considered to be R &amp; D actors, and we will certainly want to go in this direction to expand this catalogue. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-05-sejm-66-3.u23	Thank you very much. Thank you, sir. The question is answered by the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Infrastructure, Minister Marek Chodkiewicz. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-05-sejm-66-3.u24	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The government generally sees this problem. We started cooperation with contractors in 2016 with all state contracts concerning roads as well as railways. At the moment we continue this within the framework of the so-called. Expert advice. The expert board is a platform for cooperation and exchange of views, which aims to develop better conditions in relations between the investor and the contractor. What is now a big concern and an object of great interest in the Ministry is price valorization. In the forum of experts' councils, contractors raise the issue of significant price increases, which cause that contracts concluded with investors, with a state investor, still unfinished contracts, are no longer profitable for them, and can contribute to the bankruptcy of the contractor. Some of the current implementation contracts provide for a maximum value cap of 1% using indicators developed by the CSO: the index of road facilities prices and the index of bridge facilities prices. It doesn't satisfy the performers. Contractors would like to negotiate with the investor the valorisation of their remuneration in order to cover the losses resulting from the above-average increases in the cost of key materials. [[Objectively.]] However, the judicial validation investigation considers it time-consuming, costly and unsolved problem, which is the lack of resources for ongoing action and completion of tasks on time. Sometimes the public discourse raises the issue of various costs relating to possible claims on this subject, while they are very different and not supported by data. The Ministry of Infrastructure doesn't underestimate this case, but it's calm about it. We want to talk about specifics, not speculate. The collection of such data would require economic operators to verify the performance of individual contracts, specific reasons, causes and effects on time and cost. In the opinion of the Ministry of Infrastructure, the use of mechanisms prior to contractual terms and conditions, and more, because taking into account the value of payments to contractors, should be preceded by an analysis of whether this is acceptable under the applicable rules and what legal and financial consequences this will have for both parties. In view of the above, the Ministry has come to the conclusion that the following issues need to be considered: Will the changes in the amount of payments to contractors not result in financial corrections for European Union funding? Is it possible to change the amount of the State Treasury's obligations in contracts already concluded above the expenditure planned so far? Would payments be illegal public aid? This is very important. Does not the increase in the remuneration for valorisation on the part of the contract already concluded constitute a breach of the Public Procurement Law? The Resort asked the Centre for EU Transport Projects, the Ministry of Finance, the Public Procurement Office and the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection for an opinion on the possibility of applying such a solution, which would allow for the development of the position of the government party and could assist the contracting parties in reaching an agreement in this difficult situation. These works are still ongoing, the final recommendations have not yet been presented. At the same time, the expert board raised the question that price increases in the construction sector are not reflected in data produced by the CSO, whose competences include a detailed price analysis based on data received from reporting entities. Therefore, representatives of the CSO were invited to a meeting of the expert board to examine the reasons for the differences between the indicators developed by the CSO and the actual prices on the market. The GUS representatives explained the methodology used by the institution. It turned out that reporting companies inform CSO about the prices of construction works and works on the basis of contracts already concluded, not actual costs. In practice, this means that price increases... [[We've solved the deal problem.]] ...appears in CSO statistics with delay. The representatives of the CSO declared their willingness to cooperate and to be open to possible changes to the procedure adopted so far. Consequently, the CSO took action in cooperation with representatives of the road industry to change the methodology so that indicators reflect the actual prices of goods and services. As part of these activities, the CSO consulted selected contractors, reporting companies, in order to develop a new, modernised version of the so-called. Form C4, which you must know. Currently, GUS conducts research in cooperation with contractors, and if the results of these studies are successful, the GUS index, which is cited in the contracts of state investors, will reflect market trends in a better way than before. Another issue is the increase in wages for construction workers. Employee shortages are getting worse and worse. In the Ministry of Infrastructure, studies were carried out on the possibilities of facilitating employment in the construction industry, e.g. workers from selected countries of Eastern Europe or Asia. These are the needs of the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy to facilitate the employment of foreigners, including the construction industry. To my knowledge, the regulation is being prepared by the Minister of Labour. In conclusion, the Ministry of Infrastructure is open to interviews with contractors. We do not deviate from discussions, including those on price valorisation. We see problems in the market, including increases in prices of certain materials. But we're not going to panic, [[♪ The bell ♪]] The kind he's trying to induce. We are talking and we will continue to talk substantively about the particular problems that are really bothering the performers. Thank you very much.

I would remind you that you have a powerful resort with enormous possibilities, you have tools to influence the state of collective consciousness, convincing people that seals are not pests, that the Polish state wants and will defend the further existence of this species. Unfortunately, during your meeting in Mława, you stated that we are living in times of excessive sensitivity to the protection of animals. This seriously undermines your credibility as a defender of protected species, which is why we are anxious to ask: What are you going to do to stop the killing of seals and porpoises on the Polish Coast? Are you considering adopting a grey seal protection programme submitted to the Ministry of Environment in 2012, which contains very specific recommendations? Thank you for your attention.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-05-sejm-66-3.u59	Thank you very much. The question will be answered by the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Environment, Minister Małgorzata Golińska. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-05-sejm-66-3.u60	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. Speaker! I was extremely concerned to note that nearly 40 seals found on our coast at 7 found traces of human interference. We talked about this and wrote on the website of the Ministry of Environment. There was a clear voice in this matter. Seals are subject to strict species protection. The Minister's regulation explicitly sets out the prohibitions applicable to wild animals, those covered by both strict and partial protection. That's what's in it, among other things. prohibition of deliberate killing, mutilation, capture of animals. Penalty or fine shall be provided for the violation of those prohibitions. In addition, there is an animal protection law in force in Poland, which provides for imprisonment until 3 years for killing animals without complying with the conditions laid down in the Act. The relevant authorities are foreseen to carry out these investigations, i.e. The police, with which the Ministry of the Environment is in constant contact with the killing of seals, which you spoke about. By decision of the District Attorney in Gdansk to conduct seal proceedings, the District Prosecutor's Office in Gdynia was appointed. On the other hand, when it comes to accusing the ministry of inaction, I think it is worth recalling and speaking loudly about some of the actions that are being taken. The Ministry of the Environment, the Directorate-General for the Environment and the Regional Environment Directorates, in addition to posting on their websites relevant articles on protection, on how important this species is and on how to behave in the event of a seal meeting on the beach, also publish information stigmatizing scandalous cases of seal killing and inform about the criminal consequences. In addition, the environment resort is constantly involved in the field of marine environment protection, in educational campaigns on the wider nature protection. GDOŚ has also adopted a programme for the protection of porpoise, which comprehensively deals with threats to this species and methods to address these risks. In this document it moves, among others. issues of accidental by-catch of porpoise in fishing nets, the use of deterrent devices for these animals, testing alternative fishing gear which are safe for porpoise. We know that as far as this species is concerned, today the population is estimated to be about 500 and is considered a critically endangered population. As far as seals are concerned, the Ministry is working on plans to protect Natura 2000 marine areas. The adoption of these plans should effectively secure the key habitats of the grey seal in areas where this species is protected. It is worth noting that, unlike the conservation programmes for species which are soft documents, the conservation plan for Natura 2000 areas under the Regulation of the Minister for the Environment will constitute an act having the effect of acts of law which are universally applicable. These are the actions that are worth talking about, but not only. I also encourage all the parties, the organisations that you have said are very active - and thank you very much for this help to non-governmental organisations, thanks to the great involvement of which many security issues of seals are being monitored today - to engage in the development of plans that I just mentioned. The name of boosters is not a name that, so to speak, counters to approach the product, especially when it comes to young people, but not only. We want psychoactive substances, because they're psychoactive, to be treated just like drugs, and in fact, this bill is talking about it. First of all, we include the same treatment in the bill, and this also applies to distribution, because we are actually observing the transfer of sales from shops or from those places that we could identify, to the Internet or, as indicated here, to various types of anonymous points. But the main problem is drug dealers and drug dealers who, when they were dealing drugs, weren't treated like people, or were simply harder to treat them as people, so to speak, drug dealers. So, first of all, new psychoactive substances are to be subject to a similar control as in the case of narcotic drugs and psychotropic substances, leaving administrative responsibility for substitution measures, only a shift at the moment, substitution measures are mainly boosters, we move this to drugs and substitution measures will be much less. They must be disclosed, they must be indicated by the appropriate team, which has been operating for several years. The issue of supervision of substitution measures remains unchanged. On the other hand, the substances identified in them will be assessed by the Panel on Drugs and Drug Addiction. risk assessment for human health or life. This is the major change that a much wider directory of substances is being created. As a result, we will be able to identify these substances more quickly. They will be entered not as an annex to the law so far, the process takes a little longer than in the case of the regulation. We just want these substances identified as drugs. psychoactive was included there. In addition, in accordance with the provisions of the proposed act, boosters are to be treated on an equal footing with drugs. Imports, exports, carriage, intra-Community acquisition or intra-Community supply of new psychoactive substances will be subject to fines, penalties for restrictions on liberty and imprisonment, here we propose up to 5 years, which is the same as for psychotropic substances. This is so important that law enforcement authorities have reported a particular problem in the drafting of this bill in identifying... not in identifying, but in punishing these people, especially with regard to transport. In addition, the EU directive indicates that we should change the whole intra-Community market in our anti-drug law, not only in the Polish area. Following the entry into force of the amended law, the criminal liability will also be imposed on drug dealers, who will be threatened by up to 12 years in prison for the distribution of boosters, something that at the moment... from the administrative penalty of the fine, because we do not think that it was affected properly in terms of security. It should be stressed that the main purpose of this law is to include criminal law primarily in all activities relating to the distribution of these substances and to create legal instruments that allow the responsibility of persons associated with it to be held accountable. In addition, we introduce elements that allow us to plan, so to speak, to act, namely to report cases of poisoning to the State Sanitary Inspection and keep a register of these substances, because at the moment often [[♪ The bell ♪]] On the basis of information from hospitals, we know that a poisoned young man is coming, and the substance that is poisoned is not identified. As of 1 May 2018, an amendment to the Act on Financial Support for the Creation of Housing for Rent, Protected Housing, Accommodation, Hostels for Homeless, Heating Rooms and Temporary Rooms entered into force. These developments in the field of financial support for municipal construction have taken into account the demands of former housing support providers through the following solutions. Firstly, increasing the non-refundable financial support for municipalities from the Fund for Buy-in or Buy-in with the renovation of former apartments or residential building with former apartments from 30% to 50% of the cost of the project, as well as the absence of a ban on selling apartments from the municipality's housing stock where the subject of the purchase is an ex-works apartment or building with ex-works apartments. In short, the municipality can get 50% financing and, at the same time, grant a bonus that would be adequate and financingable for these tenants. The Financial Support Act defines that former works dwellings should be understood as housing which constituted before 7 February 2001 the property of state-owned enterprises and commercial companies in which the Treasury was the parent undertaking within the meaning of the Public Offering Act of 29 July 2005 and the conditions under which financial instruments were introduced into the organised trading system and public companies. Thus, the regulation does not apply to housing which is currently the property of state-owned enterprises. In addition, financial support will be provided provided that persons who before 7 February 2001 were employees of a State enterprise or a commercial company in which the State Treasury was a dominant entity within the meaning of that law, as well as the initial, apostate, spouse of the tenant, persons appointed and appointed, and persons who remained with the tenants in the actual common life, residing at the time of death with the tenant who entered into the lease relationship after the death of the persons referred to in point 1, which I listed as persons, as former employees of state-owned enterprises. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-05-sejm-66-3.u96	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, In answer to the Member's question, it is impossible to escape the need to clarify the last words that he has said, saying that the waste database is not being implemented. It's being implemented and in my speech I'll explain exactly what this condition looks like. I would like to say first of all that the Minister of the Environment - I will use the wording that Mrs Lenartowicz used at the committee meeting - was very much concerned when we came to the Minister of the Environment to ensure that the waste database was not a fake, not another very expensive Excel, but a tool that was not before and which my predecessor in one of the Senate committees said was not ready for this, neither business nor IT systems nor inspections. We have introduced a waste mass tracking module into this project to know where, when, what waste is being moved to control this system, and not to create another, very expensive IT solution that will be reduced to a simple register in Excel. This will actually be a very efficient tool using the latest technologies that are available on the IT market, including these latest innovative solutions. In order to achieve something like this, we adopted agility mode, so that we actually added more modules. On 24 January this year, according to the previously adopted timetable, the electronic register of BDO was launched by the Ministry of Environment, which is the first element of this database referred to in the Waste Act. All entities operating in the field of waste management shall be included in the register. The BDO register is, as I said, the first element of the product database and waste management. In addition to it, in accordance with the Waste Act, two further modules will also be set up from 2020 to keep records of waste and to report on products and waste in electronic form only. Fully deploying all BDO functionalities will enable effective control of the waste management market, as the President said, will contribute more effectively to the support of environmental inspection bodies in the fight against irregularities in this sector. In addition, BDOs will allow control authorities to analyse at the same time the compatibility of waste management activities actually carried out by operators with the scope declared in the register. It's also about cross-referenced data. It was also advocated by entrepreneurs, especially those who act legally in this market. In addition, due to the fact that the environmental inspection bodies will have access to the BDO, this will enable them to check the waste records, which will only be kept by entities using a dedicated module base. This should contribute to the elimination of illegal shipments of waste, together with the incorrect way in which they are recorded, which may occur in the case of traditional, paper-based records. Currently, the Ministry of Environment is working on creating another functionality, as I said, according to this agile method. These are in particular public procurement tasks. It is about developing documentation for the tender for the selection of the BDO contractor. So far, in the first stage of the preparation of the register, the IT competence of both the Ministry of the Environment we discussed and the external entity has been used. The contract concerning the register has already been awarded to a specialist company under the Public Procurement Law and in accordance with the procedures in force in the Ministry of Environment for the award of public contracts. On the other hand, a technical dialogue is currently underway prior to the preparation of the specifications of the essential terms and conditions of the contract and the description of the subject-matter of the procurement procedure for the implementation of the subsequent stages. It is planned to announce the initiation of proceedings under the Public Procurement Law Act in October this year. At the moment, we do not state a threat to the creation of a BDO within a time limit allowing, in accordance with the Waste Act, to be transmitted by entrepreneurs in 2020 through its 2019 report and to keep waste records from 2020 onwards. We also answer any questions in this area on an ongoing basis. I would also like to thank you for your interest in this subject, Mrs Kowalczyk, because it will be an efficient tool that will help the Environmental Protection Inspection in this very important process, on which, thanks to the personal involvement of the Prime Minister, Minister Kowalczyk, we have been able to speed up our work to combat pathologies in waste management. I'm sure the BDO will help here. However, I would also like to inform you that a special team is working under the direction of the Internal Security Agency and special recommendations have been prepared, which will also be announced after the completion of the work. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

You're a lawyer, I'm a historian, so you'll allow me to support myself with a note... [[Please.]] ...which I asked him to draw up a legal department to give you a precise answer to that question. The bill amending the Act on the Institute of National Remembrance was drawn up at the request of the Head of the Chancellery of the Prime Minister by the Legal Department of the National Remembrance Institute on 25 and 26 June 2018. On the basis of § 98 Rules of Procedure of the Council of Ministers The President of the Council of Ministers decided on a separate procedure for dealing with this draft law, consisting in bringing this project directly into consideration by the Council of Ministers in a circular manner. Thus, there was no obligation to carry out public consultations or inter-ministerial arrangements for this project. On 26 June, the Head of the Chancellery of the President of the Council of Ministers sent a letter to the Secretary of the Council of Ministers requesting that the bill in question be sent to the Council of Ministers immediately for consideration by the Council of Ministers in a circular manner, while requesting that the bill in question be declared urgent within the meaning of Article. Article 123 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland due to the need to take immediate action to protect the good name of the Republic of Poland and the Polish nation more effectively. This draft was immediately submitted to the members of the Council of Ministers for consideration in a circular manner and for failure to submit comments within the time limit set, i.e. up to 3 p.m. 22 on the same day, pursuant to § 92. 4 Rules of Procedure of the Council of Ministers considered to have been adopted by the Council of Ministers as an urgent project. The bill was then sent by the President of the Council of Ministers to the hands of the Marshal of the Polish Sejm, stating that the Council of Ministers considered this draft law urgent because of the need to take immediate action to serve, etc. On the basis of the article. 123 of the Constitution The Council of Ministers may declare the bill adopted by it urgent, with the exception of draft tax laws, laws concerning the election of the President of the Republic of Poland, the Sejm, the Senate and local authorities, laws governing the system and jurisdiction of public authorities, as well as codes. In short, none of these catalogues contained this amendment, so it could be considered urgent. The draft law amending the law on IPN could be regarded by the Council of Ministers as urgent, as in particular it did not regulate the system or interfere in other areas. In conclusion, this bill was prepared by the Council of Ministers in accordance with the Constitution of the Republic of Poland and the Rules of Procedure of the Council of Ministers. I hope that this is a fairly comprehensive response for the Member. [[One question.]] And one last thing. You know, I didn't expect that question from you about Volyn. It's a rhetorical question. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-05-sejm-66-3.u176	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Seym! I have the honour to present a report by the Extraordinary Committee on Deregulation on the Government's draft law on the facilitation of the preparation and implementation of housing investments and associated investments, print Nos 2667 and 2713. The aim of the bill is to support the implementation of the National Housing Programme, which is one of the priority tools for the implementation of the Responsible Development Strategy. One of the components of the National Housing Programme is the Housing+ programme, which assumes construction support for rent with the possibility of access to property, municipal and protected construction. The bill is part of a package of measures to support housing that is also available to low and average earners. The main problem of Polish housing is the statistical shortage of housing. The estimated statistical housing deficit at the end of 2015 was around 897 thousand in Poland. It must also not be forgotten that the problem of housing policy in Poland is also the lack of available housing for people whose income is too high to apply for municipal housing, and at the same time too low to borrow their own apartments. It is estimated that today in Poland there is about 40% in a similar situation. society, including mainly young people starting a career. At the same time, as research shows, the average price of square meter apartments in the largest cities has increased by 8,5% in the last year. In some towns, growth is two-digit. For example, the average rate per square metre of apartment in Warsaw has increased by almost 10% over the last year. and amounts to PLN 7821. Two-digit increases were recorded in Białystok, Bydgoszcz, Gdańsk, Katowice and Szczecin. According to the NBP report in the first quarter of this year, transaction prices increased in the original market in Gdańsk by more than 6% and in Katowice by more than 7% in the secondary market. The project is a tool to reduce the housing deficit and bring about a situation where the basic parameters determining the level of housing needs will not deviate from the average values of the developed countries of the European Union. The draft law provides for the adoption of deregulation measures to simplify and streamline administrative procedures related to the implementation of housing investments and associated investments. The deregulations proposed in the draft mainly concern the rules for the location of investments. The location of the investment will be determined by the council of the municipality, by a resolution adopted at the request of the investor. The investor's request to determine the location of the investment will be made public, with the possibility for the public concerned to comment. The assessment of the planned investment will be carried out by the same authorities and entities which carry out this assessment in the ordinary procedure for the adoption of the local plan, but the arrangements have been replaced by an opinion - except for the voivodship monument conservator, road manager and geological administration. An important solution is to link the planned investment with the findings of the study of the conditions and directions of the municipality's spatial development. Housing investments or associated investments shall not be located in areas subject to protection from localisation or construction on the basis of separate regulations, including shelters for nature conservation, family gardens and flood risk areas, unless the investor obtains authorisation under the provisions providing for such protection for the location of the housing investment or associated investment. The bill also provides for the acquisition of new residential areas, which have so far been not available for housing investments, despite attractive location, technical and social infrastructure equipment. This is the land used in the past as railway, military, production or postal services, and these functions are currently not carried out in these areas. The preparation of the housing investment will be preceded by the development of a urban-architectonic concept containing functional and spatial solutions for residential investment, taking into account the nature of the construction of the locality and the surrounding area. The concept will be developed by people with expertise and experience in spatial planning, including urban planners and architects who are currently preparing projects of local plans and construction conditions. The housing investment will have to comply with high urban standards, which are framed by the law. Investment standards secure access to the public road for residential investment, support for the necessary technical infrastructure, access to public education institutions such as school or kindergarten, access to public public transport, access to well-equipped areas of leisure and recreation and sport. The municipal government will have the right to increase or reduce the standards required of the investor by 50% of the indicators laid down in the Act, specifying, by way of a resolution, local urban standards. The municipality will also be able to further determine the number of parking spaces necessary for the operation of residential and accompanying investments and the obligation to provide access to district heating networks. Buildings built will require a decision to allow use. The competent district building surveillance inspector by an apartment investment or an accompanying investment will check compliance with the urban standards. The Act will also provide access to buildings for people with disabilities. In addition, a certain number of housing units are required to be adapted for the purposes of those persons. It is also important to extend the institutional lease formula to a lease agreement with a target transfer of ownership. To this end, it is proposed to amend the law on the protection of the rights of tenants, the housing stock of the municipality and on the amendment of the Civil Code. The proposed solutions reduce administrative burdens as much as possible, thus reducing significant investment risks. The expected effect will therefore be to carry out these investments efficiently and efficiently. Under the Act, housing investments can be implemented much faster, as the process of obtaining consent for the location of the investment will last up to 60 days, with the possibility of extending by an additional 30 days. The proposed law will be open to all entities implementing housing investments in so far as they comply with the urban standards laid down by the law. At the same time, the draft law does not exclude the possibility of preparing or implementing all or part of the investment on a general basis as laid down in the separate provisions in force. The bill will apply for 10 years. The project entered the Sejm on 26 June 2018 and on 3 July 2018 was addressed to the first reading in the Extraordinary Committee on Deregulation with a recommendation for an opinion by the Committee on Infrastructure and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy. The first reading of the project took place at the Extraordinary Commission on Deregulation on 4 July 2018. The meeting was also attended by representatives of the Association of Polish Cities, the Chamber of Architects of the Republic of Poland, Members of the Committee on Infrastructure and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy. During the meeting, all the proposals envisaged in the draft were discussed. There was a substantive discussion on the solutions contained in the project. In detail, provisions were discussed to enable the implementation of the investment regardless of the existence or arrangements of the local spatial planning plans, provided that the investment purpose is consistent with the study of the conditions and directions of the municipality's spatial development. There have also been discussions on issues relating to protection against the construction of protected areas on the basis of current legislation - in particular the area of family gardens - and so-called. the release of new land for investment. We're talking about cohabitation, generational, post-industrial areas. It also discussed the range of accompanying investments that would be possible to implement under the law and the related issue of the planning pension. There was a substantive discussion about public participation in the investment location process. There was also a discussion about a time limit which was too short in the assessment of local authorities, in which the municipality would be required to issue a resolution on the location of the investment. A representative of the Polish Towns Association asked to consider extending the period of public consultation related to the investor's request from 21 to 30 days. There are also issues concerning urban standards, both statutory and local, which will determine the municipality, and details of the urban and architectural concept. The architects' environment took part in this discussion. The Polish Towns Association called for the abolition of the so-called. Fork 50%. Several legislative and editorial amendments tabled by the Legislative Bureau of the Sejm were adopted during the process of the project. During the meeting, a formal request was submitted for a public hearing of the draft law. The proposal was not supported by the committee. A joint meeting of the Committee on Infrastructure and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy was also held on 4 July 2018. During the meeting, the joint committees gave a positive opinion on the draft law. On behalf of the Extraordinary Commission for Deregulation, I request that the draft law submitted by the committees be adopted. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

The local government will have the right to increase or reduce the standards required of the investor, but only up to 50% of the assumed indicators, specifying, by resolution, local urban standards. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-05-sejm-66-3.u223	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. As the first on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Law and Justice of Mrs Anna April.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-05-sejm-66-3.u224	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Law and Justice Club an opinion on the draft law amending the Law on the provision of public-funded healthcare. The provisions of the proposed law contain solutions resulting from the social dialogue, which results in an agreement between the Minister of Health and representatives of the Agreement of National Medical Associations of 8 February 2018, hereinafter referred to as the Agreement. The Parties to the Agreement have identified as the primary objective of their actions an increase in the availability and quality of healthcare services by increasing the funding of the public health system and ensuring better working conditions for healthcare professionals. One of the key changes is to accelerate the growth rate of financial expenditure - which is included in this law - so that these 6% gross domestic product was intended for health protection as early as 2024. Thus, in a shorter period more benefits could be financed. The Act states that a doctor who specializes in an area not defined as a priority will receive an increase in remuneration of PLN 600 per month, while in the field referred to as priority - PLN 700 per month. In order to receive a higher basic salary, the doctor will have to submit a signed commitment to work in the country for a minimum period of time - we are talking here about a total of 2 years in the next 5 years following the completion of the specialization training. The measures necessary to raise the basic salary will be transferred to the specialist training entity on the basis of an agreement concluded with the Ministry of Health. The regulations implement one of the points of agreement and address problems concerning both the creation of mechanisms to protect against the emigration of doctors educated in the Republic of Poland and the financial situation of doctors specializing in residency. On the one hand, it is expected that the solutions adopted should create a greater incentive for doctors to start specialisation while at the same time signing a commitment to work in the country at a certain time after the end of specialisation to create better prospects for securing citizens' health needs. The project assumes that programmed medical shifts are included in the cost of financing residential contracts, which I think is a very good solution because healthcare providers or specialised institutions have restricted, you can say, access of resident doctors to these shifts. This option will, on the one hand, improve the financial condition of those involved in specialisation training, which should also increase their number and, on the other hand, give the Minister of Health additional instruments of impact as regards the correctness of the implementation of this training element. The proposed law sets out the way in which healthcare providers who have concluded contracts with the National Health Fund covering the provision of health care services under 24 or 24 hours' or 24 hours' conditions, financial resources to cover the cost of raising the basic salaries of specialist doctors - that is another very important request based on the employment relationship - to the level of PLN 6750 and the cost of the increase due for the service allowance. The proposed bill is not only expected by employees, resident doctors, but above all its effects are something that will be good for patients. Therefore, Law and Justice fully supports and supports the provisions of this Law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

We proceed with the examination of item 31 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law on the conversion of perpetual land for residential purposes into land ownership rights (print No. 2673). I ask the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Investment and Development of Mr. Arthur Soboń to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-05-sejm-66-3.u260	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposal for a law on conversion of the right to use perpetual land built for residential purposes into land ownership is a response to the expectations of nearly 2.5 million perpetual users, Poles, those who today own their homes, but at the same time are perpetual land users on which these houses and apartments are located. Probably with families, the number of persons covered by this law is 8 and 10 million. This has a huge social significance and a huge importance for economic turnover in Poland. We also listened to the needs of local governments, not only Poles, but also those who exercise local authority. We consulted them to protect the interests of local governments as well. That is why I would also like to thank the local government for these arrangements and for their compromise attitude. Poles have a strong attachment to property. We respond to this and propose to Poles that on January 1, 2019 property rights of land on which Polish families meet housing needs will be for all those who today are perpetual users. This solution is part of the housing programme of Law and Justice, the most important component of which is the housing+ programme. And that's part of the apartment+. We eliminate the completely irrational situation in which people who own premises and houses are not owners of the land under this place and house. We solve the burning problems of families living in multi-family buildings, housing cooperatives or housing communities where the pursuit of land ownership under housing has faced serious obstacles for years, often impossible under existing law. We have also not forgotten the owners of single-family homes, who, in the absence of other possibilities in the previous system, built their houses on public land burdened with perpetual use with the obligation to pay for the use of this land for many years. It should be recalled that the right of perpetual use was introduced into the legal system in 1961 and was then a surrogate of ownership. In the Polish People's Republic there was a principle of indivisibility of State property which did not allow the transfer of State land to citizens. Until 1989, the only way to acquire state land for investment in cities was perpetual use. The law established at that time was not prepared for the changes that happened happily in Poland. Also in the space of Polish cities it was not adapted to the fact that after 1990 a local government was established in Poland. Then, too, the perpetual use took on a little new character. The acquisition of land from the State Treasury and municipalities was allowed. The use of the legal form of perpetual use has therefore ceased to be a kind of compulsion. However, this situation has generated further legal and practical problems, which have emerged especially in cases of permanent use for residential construction. This law was originally conceived primarily as a form of transfer of land to housing cooperatives and no one thought at the time that in cooperative blocks it would be possible to separate the ownership of the premises. The impairment of the legal structure of perpetual use is particularly evident in the case of shares in this law related to the property of the premises. The existing legislation, doctrine and case law have not yet given a clear answer to the question whether the main right in this construction is perpetual use or ownership of the premises. This discussion is not only formal but also practical, because the fate of these 2.5 million perpetual users depends on the answer. on the question of what will happen to the right of separate ownership of the premises after 99 years of perpetual use. Embarrassing citizens and giving them ownership means avoiding such complications in the future. In multi-storey buildings, there are now ridiculous situations where the inhabitants of one block pay different land charges because some have appealed and others have not appealed to the local colleges of review or to the general courts. This situation deepens the sense of social injustice associated with perpetual exploitation. The long-term practice of permanent use in residential areas proves that this law does not fulfil its systemically established function. Its maintenance is completely irrational. The statutory transformation of perpetual use into property rights proposed by Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki, once and for all, brings these legal problems and practical problems to an end, aligns the right to land with the rights to premises and houses, ensuring the strongest rights in rem: the property right of residence. Strengthening the right to land and ending with increases in annual fees will provide our country's citizens with a sense of legal stability. This is a solution that fits directly into the policy of the current government, also into housing policy under the housing+ programme. However, we must not forget that such attempts have already been made and they have often been questioned in the case law of the Constitutional Court. Our project does not repeat the mistakes of previous legislative initiatives. We have done a thorough, complete analysis of case law and we have drawn practical conclusions. In view of these conclusions, we considered that such a profound systemic change must take place gradually, in order not to overwhelm the books of perpetual self-governments, because not all of them are predictable today. This assumption allowed us to define the scope of the land needed to be converted, and we considered that it would first be the land built in single-family and multi-family buildings, together with the accompanying buildings and infrastructure enabling the proper and rational use of housing. The main objective of the project is to improve the process of total elimination of the perpetual right of use as a remnant of the period of real socialism. This objective is also pursued by the institution enshrined in this Act, i.e. delayed conversion of perpetual use when the land was built into a residential building. This solution will result in the gradual abolition of the perpetual right of use for housing purposes. Thus, the problems facing citizens today will not be re-generated. We have proposed a citizen-friendly mechanism to pay for the transformation. The current annual fee paid for perpetual use will be replaced by a conversion fee at the same amount as so far as at 1 January 2019. The added value is that the conversion payment period will be shorter than the annual fee payment period for perpetual use. In addition, citizens will no longer pay these fees as perpetual users, but as owners. There will be no need to make estimates, residents will not be surprised by jumping increases. The only possibility of increasing the value of this fee is the inflation rate, which is the only possibility. To illustrate, let me give you an example. If someone pays 60 PLN per year, by the end of perpetual use he would have to pay, say, 4800 PLN, assuming that this fee is not updated. If he made use of the 2005 Act, if he could make use of it at all, because it is very rarely possible because there must be universal agreement, he would pay for this transformation of about 1,400 PLN. According to our project, it will pay 1,200 PLN. In addition, annual discounts will be possible, while in the case of the payment of these 20 charges on the State Treasury land we have registered a 60% discount in the Act. in the first year of payment. We will also encourage local governments, as Law and Justice, to grant such discounts to their residents, so that Poles will gladly pay these fees and that this perpetual use will cease to be the topic we are talking about once and for all. Confirmation of the transformation will take the form of a certificate from the Office. This certificate will be issued either by the governor, or by the mayor or the president, depending on whether it is the territory of the State Treasury or the local government. The entry in the perpetual book will also be made out of office and free of court fees. Therefore, residents of homes and premises will not be obliged to participate or to pay additional court fees in connection with statutory derogation. Let me add that we have secured such money for perpetual books, that there will also be no problem in the work of the courts. In order to minimize the loss of the local government or something that could be called a loss, we have adopted compromise solutions. The length of this period of payment is precisely this compromise. If we compare this with the 2005 Act, the rate of payment is currently 16 and 20%. the average market value of the land. Therefore, these 20 annual charges for 1 January 2019 do not in any way reduce the revenue of the self-government from its assets, the revenue that affects the self-government for perpetual use. It is also fair to say, if this is part of our debate today, that these revenues are not extremely important for local governments today, because they are at the level of 12%. budgetary revenue. The most is in Warsaw, where they reach around 4%. all the income. Given the votes of local governments, we did not include a transformation from the law of land built exclusively by self-contained garages not related to housing. Self-governments criticized this idea, as there was a very high risk of the conversion of land under garages used for economic activities, rented and large-area commercial multi-station garages. However, I would like to reassure the garage owners, because under the current law they will be able to benefit from this mode. And if we put in place a system solution, which is our commitment and the next phase of the elimination of perpetual use, there will also be an end to perpetual use in the areas where the garages are located. As part of the planned work, we want to ensure that everyone has the opportunity to become owners. But part of the land is land that is in the use of perpetual entrepreneurs. Therefore, in the interests of legal certainty, we will consult with the European Commission on such regulations in the system solution. We will also propose a solution in the form of a different right in rem, necessary for the introduction of the Civil Code, so as to fill the gap after perpetual use. Until then, we will eliminate perpetual use for about 6%. all land of the State Treasury transferred to perpetual use and 44% all land of municipalities devoted to perpetual use. I'll say it again, 2.5. millions of perpetual users. I am absolutely convinced that this operation will go smoothly. I believe in the courts, and in the local government, and in those residents who want to benefit from the vouchers, I believe that we can do this efficiently. But these conclusions, which we will draw in the first stage, because this is 2.5 million, ultimately 4 million cases of end-of-life use, will certainly be very important in the systemic reform that I am already announcing today. And today we are solving the social problem concerning, once again, all the owners of apartments and houses who have their homes - they will finally have full ownership, because they will also have property of this land, which under this house is located under this apartment. Poles have waited a long time for this project. I am very pleased to be able to recommend today to the High House the work on this project and the adoption of this project. And I firmly believe that all parliamentary clubs will support him. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-05-sejm-66-3.u261	The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Bogdan Rzońc will be the first to speak on behalf of Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-05-sejm-66-3.u262	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On 27 June 2018, the project entered the Sejm and was addressed to the first reading on 3 July, I am talking about the print project No.2673. The government has developed and submitted a draft law on the conversion of the right to use perpetual land built for residential purposes into land ownership rights. The project involves changing the right to perpetual use to property rights and concerns land built with single-family and multi-family residential buildings. In accordance with the Government's concept, the authorisation of perpetual users will take effect by law on 1 January 2019. It follows from the justification that the project will cover around 2.5 million people, users - probably times 4 or 3, which will give about 8 and 10 million perpetual users who are waiting for system solutions to improve the transformation processes of perpetual use rights. The current legislative arrangements are insufficient, and even in some situations they do not give real opportunity to recast the law. Therefore, the proposed project is particularly expected by owners of apartments located in multi-site buildings. Owners of such premises shall be entitled to participate in the right of perpetual use of land. In such cases, the rules in force require the consent of the majority to the recast, and for various reasons it is very difficult to obtain them. For this reason, many families living in multi-storey buildings cannot obtain land ownership under the building in which the premises are located. The proposed law removes these barriers by requiring the statutory transformation of all members of the housing communities, without requiring a change of law. The Act also covers owners of single-family homes and - what is very important - also ends the time of uncertainty of citizens as to the amount of fees they incur for land under housing and houses. At present, annual subsidies for perpetual use are often increased in a stepwise way and represent a very heavy burden on domestic budgets. The project assumes that the conversion of perpetual use rights into property rights will be confirmed by a certificate issued ex officio. The transformation will be paid for. Citizens will pay annual fees for a period of 20 years from the date of the conversion. The annual fee shall be equal to the end-use charge of 1 January 2019. It also provides for the use of discounts in the form of discounts, as well as the possibility of one-off payment of all conversion fees as a total charge. For land owned by the State Treasury, in case of payment of a combined levy, compulsory discounts of between 60% and 10% were proposed, depending on the year in which the annual fee would be paid. In order to take into account the rights of local authorities, the government has provided the possibility to complete the updated annual fees for perpetual use, in which the amount has an impact on the amount of the conversion fee. In order to ensure a systematic elimination of perpetual use for housing purposes, the project proposes a ban on public land use in perpetual use for such purposes. Such a solution will in the future ensure that there are no problems that currently exist in relation to residential land devoted to perpetual use, especially in large cities. The project submitted was widely justified, agreed, consulted. It is very expected and simply provides stable law for Polish families in the field of land. Thus, this project harmonises the right to land. This is a very expected change. The Law and Justice Club proposes to support the proposed proposal. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-05-sejm-66-3.u302	Mr. Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the draft law on the conversion of the right to use perpetual land built for residential purposes into land ownership rights, print No. 2673. The introduction of new rules on the law of perpetual use is due to the need to regulate the law, the need to strengthen the rights of property guaranteed by the Constitution and to strengthen citizens' confidence in the state. For many years, users of perpetual land built in multi-storey buildings have directed to both parliamentarians and government authorities numerous complaints and conclusions pointing to difficulties in the ownership of property rights due to different property rights. In particular, a group of public land users encountered serious problems in the conversion of the land right, since the consent of all owners of premises who have shares in the right of perpetual land use is necessary for the conversion. The aim of the bill is to eliminate in the future problems arising from the exercise and conversion of rights on such land, which will increase the certainty of civil traffic. The draft law contains provisions convenient for landowners, which instead of annual perpetual use charges introduce conversion fees for a period of 20 years from the date of conversion. Their amount will be set at the level of annual fees for perpetual use with the benefit of not more than once every 3 years. In addition, landowners will be given the opportunity to pay fees once for 20 years - so-called. the total fee. Annual conversion fees for natural persons who own single-family buildings or apartments in multi-house buildings or housing cooperatives are also provided for. This gives certain advantages to owners of premises in multi-site buildings in the form of predictability of the conversion fee, which will replace the variable and the most frequently rapidly increasing perpetual use charge. The existing rules setting the standard for the protection of property rights were not uniform in nature, primarily due to their location in the basic law and the laws. The Constitutional Tribunal has repeatedly dealt with interpreting difficulties in resolving legal problems at the interface between the right of perpetual use and the right of ownership. The bill has a systemic dimension. The acquisition of ownership by a legally defined housing group will enhance legal certainty and property stability of owners of single-family homes and premises located in multi-house buildings. With the property of the house or premises from the date of entry into force of the Act will be associated with land ownership, which in the consciousness of 2.5 million inhabitants strengthens the right to housing and reduces the risk of an unjustified increase in annual charges for the use of perpetual land. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Free and Solidarni Circle will support this bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-05-sejm-66-3.u303	The Marshal of the Sejm, after consulting the Sejm Bureau, proposes that the Sejm refer to the Government's draft law on the conversion of the right to use perpetual land built for residential purposes into land ownership rights, contained in print No 2673, to the Committee on Infrastructure and to the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy for consideration. If I do not hear opposition, I will think that the Sejm has accepted the offer. I can't hear you objecting. We proceed with the examination of item 32. of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law on the amendment of the agricultural tax law, the law on local taxes and charges and the law on forest tax (print No 2668). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Energy of Mr Tadeusz Skobl to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-05-sejm-66-3.u304	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a draft law prepared by the government on the amendment of the agricultural tax law, the law on local taxes and charges and the law on forestry tax. The proposed amendment aims to organise and harmonise the country-wide rules for taxation of land through which the linear infrastructure for the transmission or distribution of liquids, steam, gases or electricity and telecommunications infrastructure is carried out. The project introduced the principle that the establishment of this infrastructure on the land of third parties from which a linear company uses it in a limited way would not affect the increase in the level of taxes. Until now, there have been no rules directly governing the principle of taxation of these land. Therefore, in neighbouring municipalities, the same land could be taxed at a different tax rate depending on the interpretation of the law adopted by the municipality. In some municipalities, the tax authorities treated the position of linear infrastructure as tax-neutral, while in other municipalities the land through which the infrastructure was carried out was considered to be occupied for business purposes, which involves the highest rate of real estate tax. This situation leads to numerous disputes between taxpayers, liner infrastructure owners and tax authorities. In recent years, this problem has been particularly evident in the case of land on the Board of State Forests on which electricity lines are located. In the last year, the tax authorities began to classify land under electricity lines in the State Forests as land occupied for business activities and not as forest land so far. According to the Ministry of Energy data, in 2017 no more than 5% Land under electricity lines in the State Forests was taxed at the highest rate of real estate tax, while in 2018 this rate may already apply to more than 40% land under the lines. The forest tax rate is around 0,5 gr/m2, while the property tax for the land on which the economic activity is carried out is 91 gr/m2, so that due to the change in the classification of the land due to the tax increases significantly. The cost of the tax will be passed on to an energy company which may include this expenditure in the tariff for the distribution service. In the whole country, in most cases the position of electricity lines on third party land did not affect and still does not change the classification of these land for tax purposes and does not result in the taxation of these land at the highest rate of real estate tax. However, there is a risk that the change in land classification will extend in the future to other forest land and agricultural land. In view of the arguments accepted by the tax authorities, the change in the classification of land may also begin in the case of land through which other linear infrastructure is carried out, e.g. Gas, heating or telecommunications. When changing the classification, no account shall be taken of the specificity of the use of land by the owners of linear devices. Linear companies use this land only to a limited extent, corresponding to transmission service. The owner of the property may normally continue to use the land for basic purposes. Forest activities may still be carried out on land under electricity lines in forests, e.g. in the form of tree plantations, while agricultural crops may be cultivated on agricultural land. Therefore, this land should not be taxed in the same way as the land occupied by a typical cubic undertaking. Nor is the objective of undertakings with linear infrastructure mentioned in the Act on the provision of services of general interest taken into account. It should be stressed that the change in the way land is taxed through linear infrastructure would lead to an increase in the prices of services provided by companies using this infrastructure. The Ministry of Energy estimates that about PLN 7 per month for the average household could increase the electricity distribution charge if all land under electricity lines were taxed at the highest rate of real estate tax. Gas, water, waste water and telecommunications services could also increase. Ground tax increases would certainly have a positive impact on the budgets of municipalities with a large number of electricity lines or other linear infrastructure, but this would have been at the expense of all electricity consumers and other media, including those of less prosperous municipalities. This would increase the diversity of the development of the different regions of the country depending on the density of infrastructure. The introduction of clear rules for the taxation of land through which linear infrastructure runs is also very important for taxable persons on the ground. As a result of this amendment, land owners or users will not be obliged to pay a higher tax solely because their land is driven by linear infrastructure facilities. The calculation of the highest tax rate appropriate to land occupied for economic activity would in particular be detrimental to owners of forest, agricultural or non-use land. It would lead to a situation where the owner himself does not carry out business activities or benefits from the establishment of a linear infrastructure and is obliged to pay a multiple higher tax on land. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I am convinced that the entry into force of the draft law is necessary. The social and economic effects of possible increases in media prices for citizens and tax increases for landowners with linear infrastructure would be higher than the potential benefits for municipalities. I therefore request a positive assessment of this law and its adoption in the form proposed by the government. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-05-sejm-66-3.u313	Thank you. Mr Andrzej Noblekhta, the Law and Justice will now speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-05-sejm-66-3.u314	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I present my position towards the government's draft law amending the agricultural tax law, the law on local taxes and charges and the law on forest tax. The need to amend these laws is due to the need to clarify the subject matter of taxation and the way in which it is classified. According to the project, the establishment of infrastructure for the transfer or distribution of liquids, steam, gases or electricity and telecommunications infrastructure on non-business land, including that infrastructure, does not change the way in which these land is taxed. In the absence of clear rules on the way the land is taxed, there is often a discrepancy in their application by the tax authorities, i.e. by the governor, the mayor, the president of the city in this case. As a result, there is a difference in taxation of these land depending on their location. In recent years, there has been a negative legal line of administrative courts that land on which the infrastructure is situated should be taxed on property tax of the amount provided for the land occupied for business activity. On the basis of these rulings, the tax authorities accept that the mere fact that they have this infrastructure is a sufficient condition for the classification of land as occupied for business activity. Such an interpretation results in taxpayers, i.e. property owners, although they do not carry out business activities and do not profit from it, being obliged to pay a higher tax only by the mere fact that they agreed to make the land available for transmission purposes. The proposed amendments to the laws are intended to eliminate doubts in doctrine and case law. The intention of statutory amendments is to introduce the principle that the location on the land of third parties of the transmission infrastructure from which the undertaking which owns the infrastructure uses it in a limited manner does not result in a change in the tax treatment of those land. Where economic activities are carried out on these land in addition to the installed infrastructure, the highest rate of real estate tax will be charged on these land on existing terms. The second objective of the regulation is to prevent an increase in the financial burden on entrepreneurs with linear infrastructure. The proposal for legislative amendments is in line with the principle of universality of taxation and the principle of equality enshrined in the Constitution of the Republic of Poland and is not contrary to European Union law. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the proposed legislative changes and proposes that this bill be referred to the Committee on Public Finance. Thank you so much for your attention.

Democracy is freedom, courage, boldness, assertiveness, subjectivity, self-respect and others. Korczak said: Democracy must be learned, democracy must be created, democracy must be fought for. It has to be made with children and for children. You have to give children rights, citizenship, protect their dignity. These slogans formulated more than 80 years ago still sound very innovative today. It's still a task and a challenge. In his most famous essay "How to Love a Baby, translated into many languages of the world," Korczak wrote: "I call for a magna charta libertatis, about the rights of the child." He mentioned the right of the child to die, the right of the child to this day and the right of the child to be who he is. In 1929 Korczak will add to this set of rights: The first, uncontested is the right of the child to express his thoughts, actively participate in our deliberations and judgments. Ten years later, he'll claim a child's right to respect. These rights Janusz Korczak placed as a task and challenge for parents, carers, teachers, educators, social workers, doctors, police officers, curators, judges, especially family, clergymen, journalists, officials and politicians. I also recall this in the context of the project Mon. The Children's and Youth's Seym. Almost two decades of the activity of the Ombudsman in Poland shows how the problems of Polish children change.
